金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

Document 060: 2026年9月经济数据点评：宏观政策调节力度加大，经济运行积极因素增多

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月经济数据点评：宏观政策调节力度加大，经济运行积极因素增多.pdf
Report source: 诚通证券
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济
宏观政策调节力度加大，经济运行积极因素增多
——2026 年 9 月经济数据点评 宏观点评
8 月以来，宏观政策调节力度加大，叠加出口保持高增长，经济运行积
极因素增多。
实际 GDP 增速回升，GDP 平减指数持续正增长。存量政策加快落地，
加上出口保持高增长，三季度实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%上升至 4.5%。三季
度名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长。AI 产
业链需求旺盛，加之能源供给扰动，物价有所改善。三季度，CPI 同比上涨
0.73%，涨幅较二季度下降 0.4 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 4.1%，涨幅较二季
度上升 0.5 个百分点；GDP 平减指数同比增长 1.5%，增幅和二季度基本持
平，连续 2 个季度正增长。
出口高增长加之内需修复，工业生产加速扩张。9 月，工业生产同比增
速 5.5%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点。其原因有三方面。一是高温多雨天气干
扰消退，加之季末效应拉动。二是出口同比增长 36.3%，增速较上月上升 11.3

个百分点，有效支撑工业生产。三是政府债券发行、新型政策性金融工具、
“六张网”等存量政策加快落地，带动相关制造业生产扩张。分行业看，装
备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快。年内，全球信用脉冲仍处在向上阶
段，全球制造业或将保持较高景气度，同时 AI 基建尚未完成，中国出口或
仍保持韧性。加之存量政策加快落地，我们预计工业生产将基本保持稳定。
存量政策效果显现，当月投资同比降幅收窄。9 月，固定资产投资累计
同比下降 7.2%，降幅停止扩大；当月同比降幅从 11%收窄至 7.2%，主要是
因为当月基建投资、制造业投资同比降幅收窄。“六张网”建设加快落实；
政府债券加快发行及使用；新型政策性金融工具实施；下调抵押补充贷款
（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，并扩大 PSL 支持领域，另增加多项再贷款额度。
受此影响，基建投资当月同比降幅从 6.9%收窄至 5.4%；制造业投资当月同
比降幅从 6.1%收窄至 1%。
受中秋错位等因素影响，消费增速小幅回升。9 月，社会消费品零售总
额同比增速从 0.4%上升至 1.1%；季调环比增速从-0.16%上升至 0.35%。消

费增速回升主要受以旧换新、中秋错位以及居民收入增速上升的影响。三季
度居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 5.3%，增速较上季度上升 0.1 个百分点，
连续 2 个季度改善。商品零售总额同比增长 1%，增速上升 0.7 个百分点。
餐饮消费同比增长 2.1%，增速上升 1 个百分点。
信用创造有所改善，保障经济平稳运行。9 月，存量、增量政策加快落
地，政府债券加快发行，信用创造有所修复，新增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多
增 177 亿元。其中，政府债券净融资 1.5 万亿元，同比多增 3469 亿元。信用
脉冲（12 个月新增社融同比增速）从-10.7%小幅上升至-10.1%。四季度，政
府债券融资或继续同比多增，信用脉冲可能继续回升，社融、M2 同比增速
有望阶段性改善。
 风险提示：新增政策存在不确定性；房地产市场止跌回稳难度超预期；
物价改善内生动力不足；海外需求出现拐点，出口增速下滑；美联储加息路
径不确定性较大。
邢曙光（分析师）
xingshuguang@cctgsc.com.cn

### Page 3

8 月以来，宏观政策调节力度加大，叠加出口保持高增长，经济运行积极因素
增多。三季度，实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%上升至 4.5%；物价改善，名义 GDP 同
比增长 6%，GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长。9 月，工业生产同比增速上
升 0.3 个百分点至 5.5%；服务业生产指数同比增速上升 0.7 个百分点至 4.8%；固定
资产投资当月同比降幅从 11%收窄至 7.2%；社会消费品零售总额同比增速从 0.4%
上升至 1.1%；新增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元。四季度，政府债券融资
或继续同比多增，信用创造有望持续改善，保障经济平稳运行。
1、 实际 GDP 增速回升，GDP 平减指数持续正增长
存量政策加快落地，加上出口保持高增长，三季度实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%
上升至 4.5%。其中，第一产业增加值实际同比增速 3.6%，较二季度下降 0.1 个百
分点；第二产业增加值实际同比增速 3.4%，较二季度上升 0.4 个百分点；第三产业

增加值实际同比增速 5.2%，较二季度上升 0.1 个百分点。
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，高于实际 GDP 增速，GDP 平减指数连续 2
个季度同比正增长。AI 产业链需求旺盛，加之能源供给扰动，物价有所改善。三
季度，CPI 同比上涨 0.73%，涨幅较二季度下降 0.4 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 4.1%，
涨幅较二季度上升 0.5 个百分点；GDP 平减指数同比增长 1.5%，增幅和二季度基
本持平，连续 2 个季度正增长。
图 1： 三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5% 图 2： GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
2、 出口高增长加之内需修复，工业生产加速扩张
9 月，工业生产同比增速 5.5%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点。其原因有三方面。
一是高温多雨天气干扰消退，加之季末效应拉动。二是出口同比增长 36.3%，增速
较上月上升 11.3 个百分点，有效支撑工业生产。三是政府债券发行、新型政策性

金融工具、“六张网”等存量政策加快落地，带动相关制造业生产扩张。

### Page 4

图 3： 工业、服务业生产增速回升 图 4： 出口拉动工业生产
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
其中，出口高增长主要有两方面原因。一是全球制造业景气度仍比较高。9 月
全球制造业 PMI 从 52.3%上升至 53%，连续 14 个月维持在 50%以上。美国 ISM 制
造业 PMI 保持在 54.5%的较高水平，欧元区 Markit 制造业 PMI 从 52.7%上升至 52.9%，
日本 Markit 制造业 PMI 54.1%。中国对主要经济体出口保持较高增速，对美国、欧
盟、东盟、日本、韩国、拉美出口同比增速分别从 34.4%、6.6%、30.2%、8.2%、
49.3%、17.5%上升至 39.6%、25.3%、52.3%、15.8%、68.2%、24.7%。劳动密集型
产品出口同比增速从 9.2%上升至 22.8%。二是 AI 产业链需求旺盛。与 AI 产业链
相关的高新技术产品、机电产品出口同比增速较高，分别为 63.7%、43.7%。其中，

集成电路出口同比增速更是高达 130.6%。
图 5： 全球制造业景气度仍较高 图 6： 各类产品出口增速上升
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
分行业看，装备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快。计算机、通信和其他电
子设备制造业，专用设备制造业，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业，
石油和天然气开采业，通用设备制造业，金属制品业，电气机械及器材制造业，汽
车制造业生产同比增速较高，分别为 16.4%、12.1%、11.7%、9.8%、8.5%、8.2%、
8.1%、6.7%。此外，受供给端约束及产能治理影响，有色金属冶炼及压延加工业生
产同比下降 5.1%。受煤矿停产整顿影响，煤炭开采和洗选业生产同比下降 3.3%。
受房地产投资、基建投资低迷拖累，非金属矿物制品业生产同比下降 2.4%。

年内，全球信用脉冲仍处在向上阶段，全球制造业或将保持较高景气度，同时
AI 基建尚未完成，中国出口或仍保持韧性。加之存量政策加快落地，我们预计工
业生产将基本保持稳定。
图 7： 装备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
3、 存量政策效果显现，当月投资同比降幅收窄
9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅停止扩大；当月同比降幅从 11%
收窄至 7.2%，主要是因为当月基建投资、制造业投资同比降幅收窄。
存量政策效果显现，基建投资、制造业投资降幅收窄。8 月以来，存量政策加
快落地。“六张网”建设加快落实；政府债券加快发行及使用，9 月政府债券净融
资 1.5 万亿元，同比多增 3469 亿元；新型政策性金融工具实施，资金规模达 8000
亿元，较上年增加 3000 亿元；下调抵押补充贷款（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，并
扩大 PSL 支持领域，另增加多项再贷款额度。受此影响，基建投资当月同比降幅
从 6.9%收窄至 5.4%；制造业投资当月同比降幅从 6.1%收窄至 1%。设备工器具购

置投资累计同比增速从 9.3%上升至 9.7%。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备
制造业，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业，电气机械及器材制造业，通用设备
制造业投资分别累计同比增长 15.8%、9.6%、1.2%、1.1%。

### Page 6

图 8： 当月投资同比降幅收窄 图 9： 当月基建、制造业投资同比降幅收窄

图 10： 工业产能利用率仍处于低位 图 11： 设备工器具购置投资增速较高
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
房地产市场止跌回稳难度较大。虽然房地产市场需求结构性改善，但整体商品
房销售面积累计同比下降 13.2%，降幅扩大 1.1 个百分点。整体库存压力较大，房
企资金压力仍存，房地产投资累计同比下降 20.6%，降幅扩大 0.7 个百分点。与此
同时，房屋新开工面积累计同比下降 24.9%，降幅扩大 0.1 个百分点；房屋施工面
积累计同比下降 12.8%，降幅仍较大。
房价分化。一线城市房地产价格有所改善，但持续性仍待观察。一线城市二手
住宅销售价格环比上涨 0.2%，涨幅扩大 0.1 个百分点，连续 7 个月上涨。二线城市
二手住宅销售价格环比下降 0.2%，降幅收窄 0.1 个百分点。三线城市二手住宅销售

价格环比下降 0.3%，降幅持平上月。

4、 受中秋错位等因素影响，消费增速小幅回升
9 月，社会消费品零售总额同比增速从 0.4%上升至 1.1%；季调环比增速从
-0.16%上升至 0.35%。消费增速回升主要受以旧换新、中秋错位以及居民收入增速
上升的影响。三季度居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 5.3%，增速较上季度上升
0.1 个百分点，连续 2 个季度改善。
商品零售总额同比增长 1%，增速上升 0.7 个百分点。通讯器材消费同比增长
31.1%，文化办公用品消费同比增长 7.9%，主要是因为以旧换新政策优化实施，加
上新一批补贴资金下发，以及新学期开学需求释放。受中秋错位影响，粮油、食品，
饮料，烟酒消费同比增速分别上升 2.7、0.1、1.3 个百分点，至 6.7%、5%、13.8%。
受油价上涨影响，石油及制品消费同比增速上升 6.2 个百分点至-0.8%。受换季影响，
服装鞋帽针纺织品消费同比增速上升 1.7 个百分点至 1.2%。此外，汽车消费补贴退

坡加之高基数，汽车消费同比下降 21.2%。受房地产市场拖累，家具、建筑及装潢
材料消费分别同比下降 3%、9.2%。
与此同时，受中秋错位影响，服务消费增速有所回升。餐饮消费当月同比增长
2.1%，增速上升 1 个百分点。服务零售额累计同比增长 5%，增速上升 0.1 个百分
点。
服务业生产指数同比增速上升 0.7 个百分点至 4.8%。其中，信息传输、软件和
信息技术服务业，租赁和商务服务业，交通运输、仓储和邮政业生产同比增速分别
上升 0.2、0.5、0.6 个百分点至 9.8%、9.5%、5.4%。

5、 信用创造有所改善，保障经济平稳运行
9 月，存量、增量政策加快落地，政府债券加快发行，信用创造有所修复，新
增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元。其中，政府债券净融资 1.5 万亿元，同比
多增 3469 亿元。与此同时，民间融资、信贷仍待进一步改善。新增贷款 1.08 万亿
元，同比少增 2100 亿元。其中，新增居民中长期贷款 874 亿元，同比少增 1626 亿
元；新增企业中长期贷款 8900 亿元，同比少增 200 亿元。
虽然社融余额、M2 同比增速分别从 7.2%、7.5%继续下降至 7.1%、7.2%，不
过领先指标信用脉冲（12 个月新增社融同比增速）从-10.7%小幅上升至-10.1%。四
季度，政府债券融资或继续同比多增，信用脉冲可能继续回升，社融、M2 同比增
速有望阶段性改善。但由于全年广义赤字规模基本持平上年、广义赤字率可能下降，

Document 068: 2045年的芯片长什么样？英特尔晶圆厂实录：芯片会长高，每一步新结构都要熬十年

Source PDF: all report pdf/2045年的芯片长什么样？英特尔晶圆厂实录：芯片会长高，每一步新结构都要熬十年.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

英特尔技术⾼管认为， 2045 年的芯⽚会继续往三维堆叠的⽅向⾛，但每⼀种新结构都要花
约⼗年去解决散热和集成问题，⼯程师⾃⼰也只有四年的能⻅度。眼前更紧的约束是设
备：全球现有⼯具⼤概只够把先进制程晶圆产量翻⼀倍，追不上 AI 每年 3 倍的算⼒增⻓。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 23 ⽇，知名科技播客主持⼈ Dwarkesh Patel 发布最新⼀期特别节⽬。他在英特尔的晶圆⼚
和研究实验室⾛访两天，与五位技术负责⼈对话，话题从环绕栅极、背⾯供电、 CFET ，⼀直谈
到英特尔与⻢斯克 Terafab 项⽬的合作。
这些对话指向两个判断。 2045 年的芯⽚会继续往三维堆叠的⽅向⻓：晶体管、连线、芯⽚之间
的连接都在向上叠，但每种新结构要花约⼗年消化散热和集成难题，⼯程师⾃⼰对技术路线也
只有约四年的能⻅度。眼前更紧的约束是设备：主持⼈提到 AI 算⼒⼗年来每年增⻓ 3 倍，英特尔
CTO 给出的估算是，现有全球设备⼤概只够把先进制程晶圆产量翻⼀倍。
英特尔研发负责⼈： 2045 年的芯⽚会往上⻓，热量是躲不开的账

芯⽚靠晶体管运算。晶体管是微型开关，开合对应  1 和  0 。给它的栅极加电压，下⾯的沟道导
通；撤掉电压，沟道关闭。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

器件缩⼩后，沟道关不严，电流会漏过去。办法是让栅极从更多侧⾯管住沟道：平⾯晶体管只
压住上⾯， FinFET 把沟道⽴成薄鳍，三⾯包住，环绕栅极把沟道切成悬空的薄带，每条裹满⼀
圈。
英特尔负责现有量产制程开发的  Chris Auth 说，环绕栅极早在  90 年代就是已知的终点： " 环绕
栅极真正是最终极的晶体管。 " 前⾯的步骤没有跳过，应变硅、⾼  K ⾦属栅、 FinFET ⼀路做下
来， " 你不会在必须做之前就去做 " 。
下⼀步是  CFET ：电路⾥的晶体管分  N 型和  P 型，成对出现，今天并排放， CFET 把它们上下
叠放，同样⾯积能多放⼀倍，代价是层间⾛线更难、热量更难散。

### Page 3

Chris 认为，到  2045 年结构会更激进。" 现在你⾯对的是⼀个悬浮在互连之中的晶体管，你就会
想：为什么不是晶体管、互连、再来⼀些晶体管、再来⼀些互连？ " 存储芯⽚已经这么做了，
NAND 闪存有了三维结构，他认为  DRAM 迟早也会⾛到这⼀步。
散热是最⼤的限制。他把限制算⼒的因素归为三件事：开关有多快，开关耗多少电，热怎么散
出去。三维器件让热量问题更突出： " 这会是⼀个限制，也将⼀直是个限制，但这些问题我们能
解决。 " ⾄于何时落地，他很保守： " 我们对技术扩展的能⻅度⼤约是四年。⾛到那四年之后，你
觉得还能再做四年。但如果谈⼗年，就很难说它会是什么样⼦了。 "
晶体管上⽅还要叠⼗⼏层⾦属连线，把它们接成电路，这⼀层叫互连，现在⽤铜。研究实验室
的  Jack Kavalieros 等⼈在探索⽤钌替代：铜线要先铺⼀层衬垫才能粘牢，线越细，衬垫占的截
⾯⽐例越⼤，钌可以直接刻蚀，不需要衬垫。他们说： " 在那些⾮常细的尺⼨上，如果能拿衬垫
的电阻去换没有衬垫，钌就开始占优势。 " 交叉点⼤约在  20 纳⽶的间距范围。他们也提醒，上

⼀个节点选了钴，现在⼜回到铜：" 当时领先的选项是钴，现在⼜是铜，将来也许是钌。 "
芯⽚之间的连接由先进封装技术开发部⻔总监  Tyler Osborn 讲解。节⽬旁⽩先点出⽭盾：芯⽚
⾯积翻倍，边缘⻓度只翻倍，⽽芯⽚之间的数据交换靠边缘进⾏。 AI 运算中，数据搬运的速度
往往⽐运算本⾝更决定整体快慢。
混合键合是解法之⼀：跳过焊点，把两颗芯⽚的铜接⼝直接对准、压合、加热，让接⼝连在⼀
起，接点就能排得更密。 Tyler 给出⼀组数字： 15 年前⼀级互连的间距是  130 到  150 微⽶，今
天量产中的  Foveros 做到  25 微⽶，实验室⾥已在  10 微⽶以下，今年  IEEE ECTC 会议上有论
⽂展⽰了  0.45 微⽶间距的混合键合器件的可靠性数据。他的评价是： " 我看到这⾥有很⻓的跑
道。 "

### Page 4

新结构晚⼗年才量产，英特尔给创新设了额度
FinFET 1989 年在实验室验证、 2011 年量产，环绕栅极  1988 年验证、 2022 年才由三星量
产，这是节⽬给出的时间线。中间要把约  2,000 道⼯序磨合到稳定，每周能在晶圆（切出芯⽚
的圆形硅⽚）上批量重复，良率（合格芯⽚的⽐例）也要达标。
英特尔的教训来⾃  10 纳⽶（⼀代制造⼯艺，业内称节点）。 Chris 回忆，当时缩放速度远超历史
⽔平，⼜要在没有新⼯具的情况下完成， " 这给流程和⼯程师带来很⼤负担 " 。他认为最该推后的
技术是  EUV （极紫外光刻）： " 当我们开始定义  10 纳⽶时，结论是  EUV 还没准备好。 "EUV ⽤
13.5 纳⽶波⻓的光把电路图案印到晶圆上，波⻓越短，⼀次能印出的线条越细。节⽬旁⽩交代
了结果：10 纳⽶原定  2016 年， 2018 年才开始制造；台积电  2019 年把  EUV 投⼊量产，英特
尔⾸个  EUV 制程  2023 年才出产品。
英特尔  14A 的外部客⼾决策正在进⾏，客⼾看重的是进度可预期。 Chris 总结： " 我们学到的

是，⼀个节点⾥不要做太多创新，因为创新越多，复杂度越⾼。 " 英特尔为此设了 " 阶段⻔ " ：新
技术从探路进⼊开发，良率、缺陷、性能必须全部达标，达不到就推到下⼀代。
同样的务实出现在晶圆⼚的会议室⾥。 Kevin Fischer 在  Hot Topics 会议上要在信息不全时拍
板：" 你会学到的⼀点是，你永远不会有完整的数据集。如果要等完整的数据集，你已经等得太
久了，已经太晚了。 " 他要判断的是 " 我们要承担多⼤⻛险 " 。
他举的例⼦是 " 外延节瘤 " 。外延是在晶圆表⾯⼀层层⻓出新的晶体，局部⻓疯就会形成凸起，让
相邻部位短路、晶体管失效。团队 " ⾼度并⾏ " ，同时试多个⽅案，既加清洗⼯序去掉节瘤，也找
源头。最⾼等级的加急批次叫  HotBox 1 ，在每台设备上都有优先权， Kevin 说： " 把⾼速公路留

### Page 5

出⼀条拼⻋道，其他⼈都让开，只有这⼀个实验可以跑。 " 代价是 " 它拖累整个⼯⼚ " ，所以⼯⼚
的⼈不愿出这张牌。
研究实验室⾥，新结构先做 " 短流程 " 样板：只做⼏道关键⼯序，测准了再拼成复杂器件。⽐如
CFET 先做上下各⼀条带⼦的简化版， " 可能还不⽐现在的并排纳⽶带有优势 " ，但能先验证叠起
来之后的接线问题。材料的淘汰同样直接，⼀种新材料可能因为 " 粘不上 " 就被判出局，或者后续
⼯序的⽓体会改变它的性质， " 如果找不到办法控制，对我们来说就是死路 " 。
英特尔 CTO ：算⼒要 3 倍，设备只够 2 倍
主持⼈提到  ASML 每年交付的  EUV 光刻机不到  100 台，要分给英特尔、台积电和  SK 海⼒⼠
等。英特尔  CTO Pushkar Ranade 承认这是限制，同时说 "EUV 或者  ASML 不是唯⼀的瓶
颈 "” 我们看到所有设备都出现了严重短缺 " 。⼀个制程⼀旦确定，设备就被锁定： " 我们不能随便
换⼀台不同的设备来做同⼀个制程。 " 他的缓冲办法是节点之间尽量保持设备通⽤， " 从  18A 到

14A ，也许  80% 的设备可以复⽤ " 。
当主持⼈问全球设备够不够把先进制程晶圆产量提⾼到  10 倍，他回答： "10 倍不⾏，⼤概  2 倍
可以。 " 主持⼈指出  AI ⾏业⼗年来算⼒每年增⻓  3 倍， Pushkar 说，除⾮整条供应链都多产，
否则增不了产出。主持⼈归纳： " 产量可以在  12 个⽉内增加，但到  2030 年不会⽐现在预测的⾼
多少。 "Pushkar 补充了周期：设备订单 "18 个⽉才能拿到货 " ，那就进⼊  2028 年，再要新建⼚
房， " 基本上要到这个⼗年的尾声 " 。
这背后是代⼯业的成本结构。 Pushkar 说： " ⼀座晶圆⼚建成后，⼤约  80% 的成本是固定
的。 " 线路和⻋站先花了钱，多⼀个乘客⼏乎不增加成本，所以每多⼀个百分点的利⽤率，都直

### Page 6

接转成收⼊。他把⾏业的集中也归到这⾥：四⼗年前有⼆⼗家代⼯⼚，现在只剩三家。
追赶台积电，他认为要补的是规模： " 我们需要追的是规模。 "” 如果你说的是技术，我认为我们
已经到位了，也许在性能、功耗或⾯积密度上差⼏个百分点。 " ⾯对主持⼈引⽤的  SemiAnalysis
对⽐，他说： " ⾸先，我不认同你的基准测试。 " 他的标准是准时：可以差⼏个百分点， " 但你必
须准时 " 。
资本开⽀⽅⾯，主持⼈给出台积电今年约  500 亿美元、英特尔约  200 亿美元（台积电已把
2026 年资本开⽀上调⾄  600 亿⾄  640 亿美元）。 Pushkar 认为这个⽐较不公平，台积电⼏乎是
所有⼈的代⼯⼚，英特尔⼤部分是给⾃⼰做，随着外部客⼾增加，开⽀会上去。
Terafab 是外部压⼒的⼀个例⼦。 Pushkar 转述⻢斯克的想法： " 我们要么建  Terafab ，要么就没
有芯⽚。 " 主持⼈指出，⼯具是瓶颈，全球硅的总量不会因此增加； Pushkar 回应，电⼒、⽔、

耗材可以扩， " ⼯具不⾏ " 。他还说，建⼀座晶圆⼚的成本每  4 到  5 年翻⼀番，⼀台  High-NA 光
刻机连同配套设备约  4 亿美元，这条曲线没法⽆限延伸。他给出⼀个思想实验：研究型晶圆⼚
⾥让⼈吃汉堡、抽雪茄，看看良率会怎样。他承认没有现有⼚商愿意这么做：" 这是⼀个厌恶⻛
险的⾏业。 "
访谈最后，主持⼈建议英特尔像英伟达锁定内存那样⼤量承诺采购⼯具。 Pushkar 答： " 如果早
知道我们今天所知道的，我们会在⼤约⼀年前就多投⼊。知道了现在的情况，我想我们会投⼊
更多。 "他还提到出售闪存业务： " 显然从后⻅之明来看，那不是最聪明的时机。 " 并认为内存的
供给缺⼝⽐算⼒更⼤。
同⼀台 High-NA 光刻机，英特尔与台积电算出两本账

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
High-NA 是  EUV 的升级版，镜头的聚光⼝径（数值孔径）从  0.33 提⾼到  0.55 ，单次曝光能印
出的特征尺⼨⼩约  1.7 倍。图案太密时，机器⼀次印不出来，就要拆成两三遍分别印，每遍都
占机器时间，也多⼀次出缺陷的机会。旁⽩算了账：⼀台  EUV 约  2 亿美元， High-NA 约贵⼀
倍，能不能值回来，取决于能少⼏遍曝光。
Chris 说，两家公司对同⼀台设备得出不同结论，原因在架构。 " 我们⽤背⾯供电，这意味着你
正⾯的间距要求会放松。 " 背⾯供电把粗的电源线迁到晶圆背⾯，正⾯只留细信号线，⾛线空间
就宽松了， High-NA ⼀次能直接印出来。他认为台积电没有背⾯供电，必须做更紧的间距，
High-NA 做不了，只能继续⽤双重曝光的  EUV 。 " 对他们来说不是很有帮助，对我们则很有帮

助。 " 但这是英特尔⼀⽅的说法。
市场此刻关⼼的是成本。台积电董事⻓魏哲家  6 ⽉在股东会上称， High-NA 尚未量产的原因纯
粹是成本， 9 ⽉⼜有报道称台积电已同意从  2030 年起在量产中使⽤  High-NA 。英特尔把它留作
14A 的选项， Chris 称会跟客⼾逐个讨论： " 它有价值，也有增加的复杂度。 "
英特尔如今也替别家公司造芯⽚（代⼯），要向客⼾提供⼯艺设计套件（ PDK ），写明这个⼯艺
能造什么、按什么规则造。 Chris 承认，英特尔⼏⼗年只做⾃家产品，没有正式的  PDK 流
程， "18A ⼀开始是没有  PDK 的 " ，客⼾反馈之后才补上， 14A ⼀开始就按这个标准做。

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好記憶體需求緊俏有利報價走升，維持「買進」 投資評等，目標價 800 元(6 x 27(F)PER)，
潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

2026/10/12 收盤價： 527.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 
611.7%，毛利率 85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利
益為 1068.9億元 ， 業外收入24.38億 ， 稅後淨利為 876.3
億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營收成
長及毛利雙雙成長受惠 平均銷售單價季增超過 六十位數
百分比，而季減中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分
比。 
➢ 展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，
且南亞科 AI相關產品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載

88.1%，EPS為 30.35 元。

品將持續供不應求。1C 產品預期於 27Q1 開始驗證，有

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

26Q3 ASP成長帶動營收毛利雙雙上升

85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利益為 1068.9 億元，業外收入 24.38
億，稅後淨利為 876.3 億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營
收成長及毛利雙雙成長受惠平均銷售單價季增超過六十位數百分比，而季減
中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分比。

26Q4合約價持續上漲  
展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，且南亞科 AI 相關產
品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價
將持續於 26Q4 上漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 88.1%，EPS 為 30.35
元。

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

New capacity announcements do not overturn our bullish view.
AI demand and increasing substrate complexity should keep the
market undersupplied from 2027, supporting further pricing and
margin expansion. We raise earnings estimates and price targets
for Unimicron and NYPCB and reiterate OW on both.
More capacity, but limited near-term relief: Suppliers are stepping up investment,

but several newly announced projects are not scheduled to ramp until late 2028-30
or beyond. Our updated supply-demand model continues to indicate undersupply
from 2027 through 2030, with tighter supply in CY27-28, which should only be
more favorable to pricing. Therefore, capacity additions announced in the past few
months do not, in our view, diminish the magnitude of pricing gains over the next
two years.
CY27 pricing should accelerate, amid tighter supply-demand in CY27-28, but

visibility on the magnitude remains limited: We see ABF substrate prices rising
25%+ in CY27, below buy-side expectations of ~50%. Our supply/demand outlook
points to undersupply next year, with larger substrates, higher layer counts, and
greater manufacturing complexity further constraining usable output. Raw material
cost pass-through was a key driver of CY26 price increases, we expect supply
tightness to play a greater role in CY27. This should allow pricing to outpace cost

inflation, driving faster margin expansion. We remain constructive on pricing, but the
magnitude and timing of increases depends on customer-level negotiations.
With pricing a segment-wide tailwind, we prefer Unimicron for its stronger CY27
capacity growth: As supply tightens, the ability to deliver incremental volume
should become a key differentiator. We favor suppliers bringing capacity online in
CY27 over those whose expansion plans contribute in later years. Unimicron’s

Guangfu II ramp should add 10-15% to capacity in CY27, compared with 5-10%
growth at NYPCB through debottlenecking, giving Unimicron greater scope to
benefit from both pricing and volume growth. Unimicron is also our preferred play
heading into earnings, as more demanding expectations for NYPCB leave less room
for an upside surprise. But we remain OW on NYPCB given its networking and AMD
CPU exposure, alongside its proactive pricing strategy.
We reiterate Overweight on Unimicron and NYPCB: Reflecting stronger earnings

trajectories, we raise our EPS estimates for both companies. Based on upside to our
revised price targets, our order of preference is Unimicron (NT$1,775 PT; 24x CY28e
P/E; 41% upside), followed by NYPCB (NT$2,000 PT; 22x CY28e P/E; 39% upside).
Potential risks: 1) Weaker demand for PCs, general servers, and AI servers; 2)
meaningful capacity expansion plans; 3) T-glass shortage; 4) CoWoP displacing ABF
substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 14, 2026 08:00 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Supply/Demand Update: Undersupply Deepens in CY27–28
Our updated model points to a wider supply shortfall in CY27–28, despite additional
capacity: Incorporating the latest expansion plans and semiconductor demand forecasts,
we now expect ABF substrate demand to exceed available supply by 9.3% in CY27 and
13.7% in CY28, versus 6.2% and 9.4% previously. This represents a widening of 3.1ppt and
4.3ppt, respectively, relative to our prior estimates. We maintain our view that CY26 is a

broadly balanced market, followed by increasingly tight supply over the next two years.
The main demand upgrade comes from other ASICs, including Chinese AI chips: We
raise our shipment assumptions for this category to 6.4mn units in CY27 and 8.7mn in
CY28, from 4.6mn and 6.2mn previously. Our Nvidia GPU and server CPU forecasts remain
largely unchanged, while we lower AMD GPU estimates while also keeping CY27 Trainium
and TPU shipments largely unchanged. The tighter substrate outlook therefore reflects

stronger demand across a broader set of ASIC suppliers, rather than higher shipment
assumptions for every major AI platform. We also raise our substrate content assumptions
for select chips based on our latest supply chain checks, lifting our substrate demand
estimates across some segments, including server CPUs and networking.
Higher supply assumptions provide limited relief in CY27-28: We now include additional
capacity announcements that have been announced recently, including two additional

Kinsus factories, scheduled to come online in CY28 and CY29, beyond the CY27 expansion
already in our model. Several larger projects, however, contribute later. NYPCB’s new site
starts ramping in 2H29, while SEMCO’s Sejong expansion begins mass production in
September 2028 and its project in Vietnam is scheduled to begin operations by June
2030. These additions increase medium-term supply but do not prevent the modeled
shortfall from worsening through CY28.

Increasing substrate complexity adds to the pressure on usable output: Larger body
sizes, higher layer counts, and more demanding manufacturing processes constrain the
output available to meet demand, even as suppliers expand installed capacity. With the
supply gap widening in CY27-28, we expect tightness to become a more important pricing
driver than raw material cost pass-through. This supports our forecast for 25%+ price
increases in CY27 and further increases in CY28, alongside continued margin expansion.

Supply-side developments
Unimicron (3037.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On September 30, Unimicron announced that it has acquired a Hukou site from
Bridgestone Taiwan for ~NT$10.089bn (~US$318mn). Based on our checks, this is for
capacity expansion plans in 2030 or beyond.
For CY26, Unimicron has announced capex of NT$53.7B.

Over the next few years, Unimicron already has several new production facilities coming
online, including:
•  Guangfu II: ramp in 2027
• Yangmei II: ramp in 2028
• Yangmei III: ramp in 2029
• Yangmei IV: ramp in 2029+
• Bridgestone: ramp in 2030+

NYPCB (8046.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On August 6, NYPCB announced that its Board of Directors had approved a capex budget
of NT$46.8bn (~US$1,474mn) to invest in building a new ABF substrate production site.
Our checks indicate that this production site is located in the southern part of Taiwan, and
will start to ramp up production in 2H29. Given the size of this investment, we estimate
that it will approximately double its capacity exiting CY30, relative to its CY28 capacity.

Kinsus (3189.TW, Not Covered)
In our previous model, we had assumed only one factory (k6b) coming online in CY27,
increasing capacity ~30%. However, recent checks suggest that Kinsus intends to bring
two more factories online, one in CY28 (k6c) and one in CY29 (k6d). This implies that
capacity would increase another ~25% in CY28 and ~20% in CY29, bringing Kinsus' total
ABF substrate capacity to almost double exiting CY29, relative to its CY26 capacity.

Ibiden (4062.T, covered by Shoji Sato)
Back in February, Ibiden first announced that it will invest capex of ¥500bn in the
electronics field planned through F3/29, for ABF substrate expansion. Sato-san's latest
view is that Ibiden will need a new factory building to install the equipment and facilities
that will be purchased with the ¥500bn. As a result, Sato-san expects Ibiden during its 2Q
results briefing (October 30) to announce plans to make a large capital investment,

including for a factory building with a long lead time, and raise its medium-term earnings
forecast (report here).
SEMCO (009150.KS, covered by Shawn Kim)
On September 29, SEMCO announced investment of KRW4.27 trillion (~US$3,178mn) in
Sejong and KRW2.51 trillion (~US$1,488mn) in Vietnam, for AI server semiconductor
substrate capacity expansion.
• Sejong: Investment period from September 2026 to May 2028, with mass
production starting in September 2028. Largest single-product investment to date,

serving as key hub for mass production of high-value-added, highly demanding
products, proactively responding to the increasing demand for AI server substrates.
• Vietnam: The Vietnamese subsidiary aims to complete construction and begin
operations by June 2030. It will be operated as a strategic hub to respond to global
volume demand and enhance production efficiency through a stable supply
system.
With this investment, Samsung Electro-Mechanics plans to actively respond to the

growing demand for high-value-added semiconductor substrates in high-growth markets
such as AI servers, HPC, and automotive electronics. In particular, this investment is being
pursued based on medium- to long-term cooperation with global big tech and
semiconductor customers. The investment includes customer funding support and
medium- to long-term volume guarantees; thus, the company expects to strengthen the
competitiveness of its FCBGA business based on a stable demand foundation.

• The site spans approximately 330,000 square meters (98,000 pyeong), equivalent

• According to a news report, LG Innotek will invest approximately $1 billion, or

(radio frequency system-in-package) and FC-CSP (flip chip-chip scale package).

initiated high-volume mass production of FC-BGA for PC CPUs in the third quarter of this
year. Simultaneously, the company is rapidly boosting revenue by securing additional
major clients. Starting next year, it plans to sequentially enter the AI accelerator and AI
server markets.
• Revenue from LG Innotek's package solution business has maintained an upward
trajectory for three consecutive years. With the package solution business

demonstrating tangible results alongside its optical solution business, the
company has strengthened its capacity to generate stable earnings.
• LG Innotek plans to rapidly expand its market share in the emerging semiconductor
substrate market, aiming to build its package solution business, including
semiconductor substrates, into a business generating US$743 million in operating
profit by 2031.
• In the future, LG Innotek plans to develop its future businesses, including

semiconductor substrates and physical AI solutions, to US$5.94 billion by 2030.
The company aims to have such future new businesses account for 25% of total
revenue, thereby completing a well-balanced business portfolio.
The expanded plant will produce major semiconductor substrates including radio
frequency system-in-package (RF-SiP), flip chip chip scale package (FC-CSP), and flip chip
ball grid array (FC-BGA). "The Gumi site will serve as a 'mother factory' responsible for

developing new semiconductor substrate technologies and producing new models and
high-value-added products, while the expanded plant in Vietnam will be operated as a
production base for general-purpose semiconductor substrates," an LG Innotek official
explained.

manufacturing facilities and related infrastructure for semiconductor products, running

through December 31, 2027.
ABF capex is stepping up, but announced investment still falls short of implied
demand. We estimate that ABF substrate makers have announced ~US$14.3bn of
investment year to date, materially above the segment’s aggregate annual capex in recent
years, although these commitments span multiple years. Our tally is not exhaustive. LG
Innotek has yet to disclose the size of its planned ABF capacity investment, while Shinko
has not quantified the additional spending required to expand capacity following its

announced acquisition of a JDI fab.
Our supply chain checks suggest that each US$1 of investment can support US$0.50–
0.60 of annual revenue at full ramp. On this basis, the US$14.3bn announced so far could
support US$7.2–8.6bn of incremental annual revenue. This compares with US$11.4bn of
incremental revenue implied by our industry forecast. We model the ABF substrate
market reaching US$20.9bn in 2030, up from US$9.5bn in 2026. Despite the step-up in

meet our demand forecast, pointing to a need for further capacity expansion over the

Morgan Stanley Research 13
The State of the Current Cycle
The ABF substrate market has gone through a few cycles over the past two
decades. We believe we are now still in the early innings of an up-cycle.
There was a prolonged down-cycle from 2012-18 as the PC TAM shrank amid the rise of
smartphones and tablets. During this tough period, companies were not able to generate
profits and had to compete on price to fill their idle capacity. As a result, during those

years, there was no or very limited capacity expansion, and capital expenditures by the
major ABF substrate suppliers were kept at a minimum.
Even as general server demand picked up, lifting utilization rates across the group going
into 2020, ABF substrate suppliers remained cautious on the longer-term demand outlook
and remained disciplined, hesitating to expand capacity. Hence, capex turned up just
slightly in 2019-20, increasing ~US$600mn vs. ~US$2.2bn on average per year in 2012-18.

This underinvestment led to sudden undersupply, as the shock from Covid-19 drove strong
work-from-home demand for PCs, as well as increased data usage and storage for general
computing driven by online entertainment. This ultimately led to a significant increase in
ABF substrate market value from 2020-22, reflected by the sudden surge in demand
along with meaningful pricing adjustments owing to the lack of supply.
During this 2020-22 period, much capacity expansion was set in motion as ABF substrate

suppliers and customers came to agree that necessary investments were needed for
future growth. Some subsidies were even given to these suppliers to facilitate this
capacity expansion. This is why capex jumped from ~US$2.9bn in 2020 to US$4.1bn in
2021 and US$5.5bn in 2022; even in 2023-24, capex remained elevated, as capacity
expansion plans continued.
Unfortunately, demand did not play out as expected. As the world came out of the
pandemic, demand for PCs and online entertainment fell sharply. This normalization of

demand, coupled with capacity expansion from practically every major ABF substrate
supplier, resulted in the ABF substrate market once again becoming oversupplied. This led
to a sharp decline in ABF substrate market value in 2023-24, as volumes normalized,
driving prices down to normalize against the new supply/demand dynamic.
Fortunately, cloud demand remained strong for general computing, and with AI demand
starting to pick up, the down-cycle this time around was much shorter (two years) vs. the

prior down-cycle (six years).
In 1H24, the ABF substrate market troughed and we saw an upward inflection since then,
but the pace of the recovery has been moderate and bifurcated. Midrange/low-end
markets such as PCs have shown moderate demand recovery, with high-end server and AI-
related end-demand recovering and growing at a faster pace. This is why we turned the
page on our bearish stance in 1H25, but did not turn more bullish until early 2026.

### Page 14

M
Idea
14
Periods Description
Period I (2010-2012): PC-centric
equilibrium
ABF substrate demand is primarily driven and dominated by CPU and GPU demand for desktops, notebooks,
discrete GPUs, with a meaningfully smaller exposure towards server CPUs.
Period II (2012-16): PC decline
and demand dilution
Global PC demand entered  a structural down-cycle as consumer behavior changed, with smartphones and tablets
absorbing more consumer time spent. This resulted in lower volumes and increased pricing competition as

suppliers fought to fill idle capacity.
Period III (2016-18): The quiet
inflection - servers and early
HPC
During these years, the PC market matured, while cloud computing started to take off. At the same time, server
CPU substrates quietly grew larger with more layers. We also started to see early accelerators being adopted in
high performance computing.
Period IV (2018-2022): WFH
demand driven structural
shortage
The Covid-19 pandemic drove massive work from home demand for consumer electronics and cloud computing.

This sudden surge in PC demand and increased time spent on gaming and online entertainment, bundled with a
continuous increase in substrate package size and layer count, resulted in a structural shortage, which also resulted
in longer lead times and raised prices. It was during this period (2021-22) that ABF suppliers globally started to
invest in new capacity.
Period V (2022-2024): Post-
pandemic correction and new
capacity arrival
As the world came out of the Covid-19 pandemic, WFH demand faded and  PC and cloud computing demand were

negatively affected. But at the same time, the new investments and capacity additions were just starting to slowly
come online, resulting in a drop in utilization rates and pricing.
Period VI (2024-2026):
Bifurcated recovery
PC demand normalized at the pre-Covid levels, with some replacement demand driven by Windows upgrade, while
the recovery and growth of AI and general server demand for cloud picks up at a meaningfully higher growth
trajectory.

Period VII (2026-30): AI diffusion Demand for AI proliferates from training to inference, driving ABF substrate market growth to re-accelerate over the
next five years after a two-year down-cycle following the Covid-19 pandemic. This drives increased demand for AI
computing, networking, and cloud computing, while demand for PCs remains soft.

Breaking down ABF substrate end demand
The end market mix for ABF substrates has shifted dramatically over the past decade. PCs

were once the dominant demand driver, accounting for approximately 70% of global ABF
substrate market value in 2015, supported primarily by PC CPUs and GPUs. Since then,
however, the industry's demand profile has undergone a structural transformation. We
estimate that PC-related applications represented only ~23% of total ABF substrate
market value in 2025, and this share is expected to decline further to below 10% by 2030.
In contrast, demand from servers, AI GPUs, AI ASICs, and networking applications has

expanded rapidly. We estimate that these segments collectively accounted for only
around 10% of the ABF substrate market in 2015, but their share increased to ~60% by
2025. Looking ahead, we expect they will represent more than 80% of total ABF
substrate market value by 2030.
In our view, this structural shift in end-market demand has been the primary driver of ABF
substrate industry growth over the past decade and will become even more pronounced

over the next five years. As AI infrastructure deployment accelerates, we expect continued
expansion in server, AI accelerator, and networking demand to drive meaningful
acceleration in ABF substrate market growth.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target increases ~21% to NT$1,775 (from NT$1,465), driven by our
increased earnings estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have
returned to a growth track from 2026 onward and will show stronger  growth in 2027,
driven by the high-end ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply and Unimicron's
dominant supply share in AI ASICs and also server CPUs, which puts it in a favorable

position to benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and see growth
accelerating from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs, and networking chips. New
technologies such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T should also increase the
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, which adds to the under-capacity as

usable output decreases.
Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value Unimicron – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm as it takes into account cost of equity. Our key assumptions
include:
• Cost of equity of 9.2% (unchanged)
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 14% (slightly lowered from 15%)

• Terminal growth rate of 3% (unchanged, similar to the rest of the companies under
our tech hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,030 and NT
$595, respectively.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~29%, to NT$2,000, reflecting our raised earnings
estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have returned to a growth
track from 2026 onward and will show stronger growth in 2027, driven by the high-end
ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply, NYPCB's dominant supply share for
networking chips, and its proactive pricing strategy, which puts it in a favorable position to

benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and growth should
accelerate from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs and networking chips. New
technologies, such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T, should also increase
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, adding to the overall undercapacity
as usable output decreases.

Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value NYPCB – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. Key assumptions
(all unchanged):
• Cost of equity of 9.3%;
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 17%  –  fair given our analysis of the substrate market

over the next couple of years;
• Terminal growth rate of 3% (similar to the rest of the companies under our tech
hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,575 and NT
$1,020, respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.  
Buy 
Catalyst Watch: Upside, expires 26-NOV-26

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

### Page 4

Dynapack International (3211.TWO)
19 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com 
       廖婉婷 TINA_LIAO@uni-psg.com

臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元 ， 改寫單月新高，3Q26 營收 646.24 
億元，優於研究部原估的 607.09 億元，主因 AI 相關業務出貨
優於預期 ， 加上手機客戶進入新品旺季 ， 研究部據此上修 3Q26 稅
後 EPS 至 3.84 元（原估 3.47 元）。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺
季延續， 加上 GPU 與 ASIC 新一代主板開始放量 ，營收與獲利將
續創新高 。2027 年伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成
為主流， 營收可望各再增逾百億元；IC 載板亦受惠供給持續吃緊
及新產能陸續開出 ， 帶動 AI 相關營收占比突破三成 。 綜上所述 ，
研究部上修臻鼎 2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 至 12.11 元（+99.6% 
YoY，原估 11.81 元）及 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，原估 22.91 
元）。基於臻鼎產品橫跨軟板、類載板、HDI、HLC 與 IC 載板，
可為客戶提供一站式解決方案 ， 並提前掌握關鍵材料與設備產能 ，
在高階 PCB 供給吃緊下擴產得以大量開出搶奪市占，研究部維
持以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎及 28 倍本益比，目標價上調至 
730 元，維持買進投資評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 9 月營收改寫新高，上修 2H26 獲利預估 
臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元（+27.5% MoM、+36.1% YoY），改寫
單月新高 ， 其中伺服器 、 光模塊與 IC 載板合計營收年增逾三倍 ，
行動通訊亦呈雙位數月增 與年增 。3Q26 營收 646.24 億元
（+33.4% QoQ、+36.4% YoY），達研究部原估的 106.4%，明顯優
於預期 ， 主因 GPU 新一代主板進入量產 、1.6T 光模塊出貨放大 ，
加上手機客戶進入新品旺季。考量 AI 相關產品占比提升，研究
部預估 3Q26 毛利率為 25.5%（+2.4ppt QoQ、+3.5ppt YoY，原
估 24.7%），稅後 EPS 上修至 3.84 元（+81.9% QoQ 、+81.9% 
YoY，原估 3.47 元） 。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺季延續，加上 GPU 
與 ASIC 新一代主板放量，研究部預估 4Q26 營收 736.13 億元
（+13.9% QoQ、+29.4% YoY），稅後 EPS 4.75 元（+23.8% QoQ、
+70.4% YoY，原估 4.86 元），營收與獲利續創新高。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. AI 伺服器主板成為最主要成長動能 
AI 伺服器主板隨 GPU 與 ASIC 平台升級，晶片封裝尺寸、I/O 
數與傳輸速率同步提升，主板須在有限面積內完成更多高速訊號
的扇出與繞線。 傳統 HLC 以一次壓合搭配機械鑽孔與背鑽 ，層間
連接密度已近極限；高階 HDI 則以多次壓合與雷射盲孔逐層堆
疊，線路更細、訊號路徑更短，可降低高速傳輸損耗，帶動 GPU 
平台 OAM 等主板設計由 HLC 轉向 HDI。HDI 須投入大量雷射鑽
孔機與多次壓合產能，在設備與材料交期拉長下，全球高階  AI 
PCB 產能缺口約 15–20%， 訂單已排至 2028 年，2027–2028 年
產品報價亦將持續上調。臻鼎提早取得全球逾五成高階雷射鑽孔
機產能 ， 在建 22 座新廠中半數投入 HDI、 伺服器與光模塊產品 ，
在同業擴產受限下得以持續承接訂單，帶動伺服器 HDI 比重由 
1H26 的約五成於 2H26 明顯提升；繼 ASIC 專案今年大量出貨
後，GPU 平台新一代主板亦自 4Q26 起放量。研究部預估臻鼎伺
服器營收 2026、2027 年各增加逾 100 億元 ， 為未來兩年最主要
成長動能。

3. 臻鼎可望躍居 1.6T 光模塊 PCB 龍頭 
AI 伺服器傳輸速率由 800G 升級至 1.6T，單通道速率倍增使線
路須更細 、 阻抗控制更精準 ， 傳統減成法已難以達到 ， 光模塊 PCB 
因此改採 mSAP 製程。臻鼎過去為手機類載板主要供應商，具備 
mSAP 大量生產經驗 ； 在高階設備交期拉長 、 關鍵材料供給吃緊下 ，
臻鼎已掌握市場上多數高階設備產能並持續擴充，同時提前鎖定
上游材料，得以在淮安與泰國同步擴充光模塊產能，研究部推估
臻鼎可望在 2027 年 1.6T 世代成為全球光模塊 PCB 龍頭。隨 
1.6T 出貨占比由 2026 年的約五成提升至 2027 年逾七成，研
究部預估臻鼎光模塊營收由 2025 年約 9 億元躍升至 2026 年
逾 100 億元，2027 年再增至逾 250 億元。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. ABF 與 BT 同步吃緊，新產能與轉單推升載板營收 
AI 晶片封裝面積與層數持續放大，ABF 載板用量與製程難度同
步提升，惟新廠建置期長、上游材料吃緊，供需缺口持續擴大；
同業亦將部分 BT 產能轉往 ABF，使 BT 載板同步出現缺貨，兩
者報價自下半年起持續上漲。臻鼎 ABF 載板技術規格已達最大 
158×158mm、 最高 28 層 ， 深圳一廠 1Q26 轉盈並將於年底滿載 ，
二廠 1Q27 開始貢獻，第二園區 2028 年起約每年開出一座新
廠，且新產能幾乎全由國際客戶預先包下，高雄廠亦已量產先進
封裝相關載板。BT 載板雖無新建廠計畫， 但近期承接同業轉單並
調漲報價 ， 加上產品與客戶組合持續優化 ， 可望維持雙位數成長 。
隨 ABF 產能接續開出，研究部預估 ABF 占載板營收比重將由 
2025 年的 35% 提升至 2027 年約七成，IC 載板營收 2026、
2027 年分別成長 99.2% 及 89.0%。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

5. 高階新產能陸續開出，產品組合優化推升毛利率 
臻鼎 2026 年資本支出逾 800 億元，2027 年維持相當水準，廠
房數將由目前的 28 座擴增至 2030 年底的 59 座。新產能自 
2027 年起陸續開出：淮安 6 座新廠於 2026 年底完工後進入裝
機，HD 園區預計 3M27 投產、2Q27 大批量產；泰國新廠於 4Q26 
起試產，1H27 進入小量生產 ； 深圳第三園區與載板第二園區則預
計自 2028 年起接棒 （表二） 。新廠初期稼動率與良率仍在爬坡，
加上 2027 年折舊費用預計較 2026 年再增加 20% 至 30%，短
期將稀釋毛利率；惟新增產能以 AI 伺服器、光模塊與 IC 載板
為主，隨 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 2027 
年突破三成，研究部預估臻鼎毛利率將由 2026 年的 24.5% 提
升至 2027 年的 29.3%。

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  5

6. 上修 2026、2027 年獲利預估 
2026 年 AI 相關業務為主要成長動能 ，研究部預估伺服器 、光模
塊與其他營收成長 269.0%，IC 載板成長 99.2%，行動通訊受惠
摺疊機新品成長 6.9%，電腦消費則與 2025 年持平，AI 相關營
收占比將由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 25.9%。研究部預估 2026 
年營收 2,273.96 億元（+24.6% YoY，原估 2,267.70 億元），
毛利率由 24.1% 上修至 24.5%（+4.7ppt YoY ），稅後 EPS 由 
11.81 元上修至 12.11 元（+99.6% YoY）。 
展望 2027 年，伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成為
主流，營收合計成長逾一倍，IC 載板受惠 AI 客戶訂單放量、
ABF 比重提升及報價調漲，成長 89.0%，行動通訊與電腦消費則
分別維持中、低個位數成長。在高毛利產品占比提升、費用率隨
營收規模擴大回落，抵銷新廠折舊增加下，研究部預估 2027 年
營收 3,158.99 億元（+38.9% YoY，原估 3,083.20 億元），毛
利率提升至 29.3%（+4.8ppt YoY，原估 28.1%） ，營益率提升至 
17.9%（原估 14.8%） ， 稅後 EPS 上修至 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，
原估 22.91 元）。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

司 認 可 之 資 料來 源 ， 並 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 但 不 保 證 其 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 ， 報
告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

Document 237: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

析师 ” 第⼀名，连续 10 年⼊围前三名。在⼗年间持续获得掌管数万亿资产的⾦融机构集体投票⽀
持，徐⼩庆是中国 “ 最懂利率 “ 的⼏个⼈之⼀。2013 年，徐⼩庆加⼊百亿私募基⾦敦和资管担任

变动影响资产轮动的前沿研究，⽤于投资实践指引资产配置。早在 2018 年，华尔街⻅闻就邀请

效的中国特⾊ “ 利率为锚 ” 的资产轮动与配置⽅法。这是他从业 20 多年间，亲历中国债券市场 5 轮
⽜熊轮动，对中国利率周期影响资产轮动进⾏系统跟踪研究的成果。这⻔课程⽬前已获得上万
名⽤⼾付费订阅，⼀直是⽤⼾评价最⾼和回看复习最多的课程。

Document 253: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Hungarian and Czech growth, plus CCA rate decisions

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Hungarian and Czech growth, plus CCA rate decisions.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-23

### Page 1

Hungary and the Czech Republic will release third-quarter GDP
estimates next week, while Uzbekistan and Azerbaijan
announce their latest monetary policy decisions

Peter Virovacz, David Havrlant and Dmitry Dolgin
Hungary's growth picture remains mixed
Hungary: Flash GDP to provide ﬁrst look at
third-quarter growth
Friday brings the release of the preliminary Q3 GDP estimate. We
expect agriculture to have weighed heavily on growth in the third
quarter, but industry – mostly driven by the new export capacities –
should largely oﬀset this. Services will remain a major positive
contributor.
As this is only a ﬂash estimate, the data will provide few details.
Even so, annual growth should remain robust despite the quarterly
slowdown. We therefore maintain our full-year growth forecast of
1.9% and continue to expect growth to pick up in the ﬁnal quarter
of the year.

### Page 2

Czech Republic: Conﬁdence likely to
weaken as growth softens
Monday brings the release of October conﬁdence indicators, and on
Friday we'll get the preliminary 3Q26 GDP estimate. We expect
economic performance to have weakened further on an annual
basis in 3Q26, but quarter-on-quarter growth should have picked
up thanks to a pre-stocking eﬀect triggered by the Hormuz conﬂict.
Large numbers of people submitted new orders when it became
clear that prices would rise and products would become more

scarce.
That said, we assume this positive eﬀect will fade at the year's turn.
Both business and consumer conﬁdence likely deteriorated in
October, reﬂecting rising prices, the bond-market selloﬀ, the
propensity for tighter monetary policy, and the crisis in France.
CCA: Uzbekistan and Azerbaijan central
banks expected to stay on hold
Wednesday brings the Central Bank of Uzbekistan's latest interest
rate decision. We expect the CBRU to keep the policy rate

unchanged at 14.00%, although a 50bp cut remains a close
alternative. A hold appears appropriate given an increasingly pro-
inﬂationary and hawkish global backdrop, some recent weakening
in the soum and still-strong domestic demand. At the same time,
the case for easing continues to build, as core inﬂation has recently
stabilised at 5.5% YoY, not far above the 5% target, real interest
rates remain high, and domestic bank funding continues to
materially outperform lending growth, pointing to restrictive

monetary conditions. We therefore see a hold as a tactical pause
before gradual easing in the medium term.
The Central Bank of Azerbaijan announces its latest interest rate
decision on Thursday. We expect the CBA to keep the reﬁnancing
rate unchanged at 6.50%, as inﬂation remains within the 4±2%
target range. Headline CPI eased to 5.5% YoY in September, arguing
against tightening. However, CPI remains relatively close to the
upper end of the target range, while domestic producer-price

Document 289: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.
Figure  3-1 Export Value Index (YoY) Figure 3-2 Number of Foreign Visitors to Japan and
Real Inbound Consumption

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

The Japan Research Institute, Limited- ８ -
